[\ell]
the following convention is adopted,

[\ell]~\equiv~\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}~(s_{L},s_{R})&\text{for $s_{L}=s_{R}$}\\
\\
~(s_{L},s_{R})\oplus(s_{R},s_{L})&\text{for $s_{L}\neq s_{R}$}\end{array}\right..

(4.55)

For the spin wave function at the standard momentum
k^{\mu}=(m,\mathbf{0})
, consider a massive particle with spin-
s
.
Its associated IRREP
[\ell]=(s_{L},s_{R})=[l]\otimes[r]
is defined as

\displaystyle u^{\ell}_{\sigma}({k};s)\equiv u^{\ell}_{\sigma}({k};[\ell],s)

\displaystyle=u^{lr}_{\sigma}({k};(s_{L},s_{R}),s),

(4.56)

\displaystyle\bar{u}_{\ell}^{\sigma}({k};s)\equiv\bar{u}_{\ell}^{\sigma}({k};[\ell],s)

\displaystyle=\bar{u}_{lr}^{\sigma}({k};(s_{L},s_{R}),s),

where
l=-s_{L},-s_{L}+1,\cdots,s_{L}
and
r=-s_{R},-s_{R}+1,\cdots,s_{R}
correspond to the IRREP indices of
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{L}
and
\mathrm{SU}(2)_{R}
, respectively.
Note that, in our convention, the placement of the indices on the wave functions
u
and
\bar{u}
differs from Ref.
[
54
]
.

The spin wave functions
u^{lr}_{\sigma}({k};(s_{L},s_{R}),s)
and
\bar{u}_{lr}^{\sigma}({k};(s_{L},s_{R}),s)
are essentially the CGCs
5
5

5

A more detailed derivation can be found in Section VII of Chapter V in Ref.
[
80
]
.
, which can be written as

u^{lr}_{\sigma}({k};(s_{L},s_{R}),s)=C^{s_{L},\sigma_{l};s_{R},\sigma_{r}}_{s,\sigma},\quad\bar{u}_{lr}^{\sigma}({k};(s_{L},s_{R}),s)=C_{s_{L},\sigma_{l};s_{R},\sigma_{r}}^{s,\sigma}.

(4.57)

The spin wave functions satisfy the orthonormality relation

\bar{u}^{\sigma_{1}}_{\ell}({k};s)u^{\ell}_{\sigma_{2}}({k};s)=\delta_{\sigma_{1}}^{\sigma_{2}}.

(4.58)

From Eq. (
3.69
), the spin wave function in any frame can be written as

\displaystyle u^{lr}_{\sigma}({p};(s_{L},s_{R}),s)=

\displaystyle D^{l}_{\,\,l^{\prime}}(L(\mathbf{p}))D^{r}_{\,\,r^{\prime}}(L(\mathbf{p}))u^{l^{\prime}r^{\prime}}_{\sigma}({k};(s_{L},s_{R}),s),

(4.59)

\displaystyle\bar{u}_{lr}^{\sigma}({p};(s_{L},s_{R}),s)=

\displaystyle D^{l^{\prime}}_{\,\,l}(L^{-1}(\mathbf{p}))D^{r^{\prime}}_{\,\,r}(L^{-1}(\mathbf{p}))\bar{u}_{l^{\prime}r^{\prime}}^{\sigma}({k};(s_{L},s_{R}),s).

From Eqs. (
4.12
) and (
4.19
), it follows that the spin wave functions in the left- and right-handed representations can be constructed from spinors.
For spin-
\frac{1}{2}
, the spin wave functions can be written as

u_{\sigma}^{l0}(p;(\frac{1}{2},0),\frac{1}{2})\to U^{l}{}_{\alpha}\lambda^{\alpha}_{I},\qquad u_{\sigma}^{0r}(p;(0,\frac{1}{2}),\frac{1}{2})\to\tilde{\lambda}^{\dot{\alpha}}_{I},

(4.60)

\bar{u}^{\sigma}_{0r}(p;(\frac{1}{2},0),\frac{1}{2})\to\tilde{\lambda}^{I}_{\dot{\alpha}},\qquad\bar{u}^{\sigma}_{l0}(p;(0,\frac{1}{2}),\frac{1}{2})\to-(U^{-1})^{\alpha}_{\,\,l}\lambda^{I}_{\alpha},

(4.61)

where
U^{l}_{\,\,\alpha}
is a constant matrix that implements the change of notation between the indices
(l,r)
and the spinor indices
(\alpha,\dot{\alpha})
.
In the
(l,r)
notation, the four-dimensional Pauli matrices are
(\sigma_{\mu})^{l}{}_{r}
, whereas in the spinor notation they are denoted by
(\sigma_{\mu})_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}
.
With this convention, one chooses

U^{l}{}_{\alpha}=\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix},\qquad(U^{-1})^{\alpha}_{\,\,l}=\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
-1&0\end{pmatrix}.

(4.62)

More generally, the spin wave functions with arbitrary spin-
s
can be constructed from basic spinors and written as

\bar{u}^{\{I\}}_{\ell}(p;s)\equiv\left(|p\rangle^{2s_{L}}[p|^{2s_{R}}\right)^{\{I\}}_{\ell},\qquad u_{\{I\}}^{\ell}(p;s)\equiv\left(|p]^{2s_{R}}\langle p|^{2s_{L}}\right)_{\{I\}}^{\ell},

(4.63)

where
[s_{L},s_{R}]
denotes the irreducible representation of the Lorentz group.
For simplicity, the constant matrices
U
that relate the
(l,r)
indices to the spinor indices
(\alpha,\dot{\alpha})
, as well as the normalization factors of the spin wave functions, are omitted here.

Starting from the definition of
the spin wave functions, all the spin wave functions are written as functions of the parent momentum and the spin quantum numbers of the individual particles, in Eq. (
4.63
). The rewritten amplitude is:

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\{I_{3}\}}^{\{I_{1}\}\{I_{2}\}}(p_{3},p_{1},p_{2};L,S)\equiv

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\ell_{3}}^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}(p_{3},p_{1},p_{2};L,S)(\tau^{2s_{1}}_{1})^{\{I_{1}\}}_{\{J\}}(\tau^{2s_{2}}_{2})^{\{I_{2}\}}_{\{K\}}\times

(4.64)

\displaystyle u_{\{I_{3}\}}^{\ell_{3}}(p_{3};s_{3})\bar{u}_{\ell_{1}}^{\{J\}}(p_{3};s_{1})\bar{u}_{\ell_{2}}^{\{K\}}(p_{3};s_{2}),

where the factor
\tau^{\{I\}}_{\{J\}}
can be obtained from Eq. (
4.44
). The role of
\tau^{\{I\}}_{\{J\}}
is analogous to the little group rotation part in Eq. (
3.127
).
For the coupling structure
\Gamma_{\ell_{3}}^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}
, its definition is the same as in Eq. (
3.100
), and the covariant projection tensor can be written as

\displaystyle P_{\ell_{3}}^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}(p_{3};s_{3},s_{1},s_{2})

\displaystyle=\bar{u}^{\{I_{3}\}}_{\ell_{3}}(p_{3},s_{3})u_{\{I_{1}\}}^{\ell_{1}}(p_{3},s_{1})u_{\{I_{2}\}}^{\ell_{2}}(p_{3},s_{2})C_{s_{3},\{I_{3}\}}^{s_{1},\{I_{1}\};s_{2},\{I_{2}\}}

(4.65)

\displaystyle=(|3\rangle^{2s_{3L}}[3|^{2s_{3R}})^{\{I_{3}\}}_{\ell_{3}}(|3]^{2s_{1R}}\langle 3|^{2s_{1L}})_{\{I_{1}\}}^{\ell_{1}}(|3]^{2s_{2R}}\langle 3|^{2s_{2L}})_{\{I_{2}\}}^{\ell_{2}}C_{s_{3},\{I_{3}\}}^{s_{1},\{I_{1}\};s_{2},\{I_{2}\}},

where the pair
[s_{iL},s_{iR}]\ (i=1,2,3)
are the Lorentz group irreducible representation of the
[\ell_{i}]
.

For the orbital part
t_{\ell_{L}}^{L}
, one has

t_{\ell_{L}}^{L}(p_{3};p_{1})=\mathcal{Y}^{L}_{\{I\}}(p_{3},p_{1})\times(|3\rangle^{L}[3|^{L})^{\{I\}}_{\ell_{L}},

(4.66)

where
\mathcal{Y}^{L}_{\{I\}}
has already been defined in Eq. (
4.40
).

Having established the explicit structures, the covariant projection tensor amplitude in Eq. (
3.127
) and the canonical-spinor amplitude in Eq. (
4.64
) can now be compared.
In any frame, the two amplitudes can be written as

\displaystyle A_{\sigma_{3}}^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}(p_{3},p_{1},p_{2};L,S)=

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\ell_{3}}^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}(k_{3},p_{1}^{*},p_{2}^{*};L,S)u^{\ell_{3}}_{\sigma_{3}}(k_{3};s_{3})

(4.67)

\displaystyle\times\underbrace{D^{\ell^{\prime}_{1}}_{\,\,\ell_{1}}(R_{31}^{-1})\bar{u}_{\ell^{\prime}_{1}}^{\sigma_{1}}(k_{1};s_{1})}_{\text{LG + wave function}}\ \underbrace{D^{\ell^{\prime}_{2}}_{\,\,\ell_{2}}(R_{32}^{-1})\bar{u}_{\ell^{\prime}_{2}}^{\sigma_{2}}(k_{2};s_{2})}_{\text{LG + wave function}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\{I_{3}\}}^{\{I_{1}\}\{I_{2}\}}(p_{3},p_{1},p_{2};L,S)=

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\ell_{3}}^{\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}(p_{3},p_{1},p_{2};L,S)u^{\ell_{3}}_{\{I_{3}\}}(p_{3};s_{3})

\displaystyle\times\underbrace{\tau_{1\{J\}}^{\{I_{1}\}}\bar{u}^{\{J\}}_{\ell_{1}}(p_{3};s_{1})}_{\text{LG + wave function}}\,\underbrace{\tau_{2\{K\}}^{\{I_{2}\}}\bar{u}^{\{K\}}_{\ell_{2}}(p_{3};s_{2})}_{\text{LG + wave function}}.

By comparison, the canonical-spinor based covariant projection tensor amplitude can keep covariance explicit, and it does not require boosting the event back to the two body COM frame. Moreover, the covariant projection tensor amplitude rewritten in terms of the canonical-spinor variables is equivalent to the canonical-spinor amplitude. This equivalence originates from fully contracting all Lorentz indices in the covariant projection tensor amplitude rewritten with canonical-spinor variables, and the result of this contraction is exactly the canonical-spinor amplitude. This point also guarantees that, when it is returned to the COM frame, the orbital and spin parts are completely separated, and it is consistent with the traditional-
LS
amplitude.

4.3
Examples of canonical-spinor amplitude calculation

In this subsection, several explicit examples of canonical-spinor amplitude calculations are presented. Using Eqs.(
4.19
) and (
4.20
), the following basic
spinor structures are obtained:

p_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}=|p_{\alpha I}\rangle[p^{I}_{\dot{\alpha}}|,\quad p_{\dot{\alpha}\alpha}=|p^{\dot{\alpha}I}]\langle p_{I}^{\alpha}|,

(4.68)

\langle p_{I}q^{J}\rangle=-\langle q^{J}p_{I}\rangle,\quad[p_{J}q^{I}]=-[q^{I}p_{J}],

(4.69)

\langle p^{I}p_{J}\rangle=m\delta^{I}_{J},\quad[p_{J}p^{I}]=m\delta^{I}_{J},\quad|p_{I}\rangle\langle p^{I}|=m\delta^{\beta}_{\alpha},\quad|p^{I}][p_{I}|=m\delta^{\dot{\beta}}_{\dot{\alpha}},

(4.70)

p|p^{I}]=-m|p^{I}\rangle,\quad p|p_{I}\rangle=-m|p_{I}].

(4.71)

For on-shell three-point amplitudes, the following conditions must be satisfied:

p_{3}=p_{1}+p_{2},\quad(p_{i})_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}(p_{j})^{\dot{\alpha}\beta}+(p_{j})_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}(p_{i})^{\dot{\alpha}\beta}=2(p_{i}\cdot p_{j})\delta^{\beta}_{\alpha},\quad p_{i}^{2}=m_{i}^{2}\quad(i,j=1,2,3).

(4.72)

Here we choose an arbitrary reference frame and define the canonical-spinor variables for particle-1, particle-2, and particle-3 as

\displaystyle|1_{I}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sqrt{m_{1}}\begin{pmatrix}c^{2}_{1}e^{\frac{\eta_{1}}{2}}+|s_{1}|^{2}e^{-\frac{\eta_{1}}{2}}&c_{1}s^{*}_{1}(e^{\frac{\eta_{1}}{2}}-e^{-\frac{\eta_{1}}{2}})\\
c_{1}s_{1}(e^{\frac{\eta_{1}}{2}}-e^{-\frac{\eta_{1}}{2}})&|s_{1}|^{2}e^{\frac{\eta_{1}}{2}}+c^{2}_{1}e^{-\frac{\eta_{1}}{2}}\end{pmatrix}_{\alpha I}=\begin{pmatrix}a_{1}&b_{1}\\
b^{*}_{1}&c_{1}\end{pmatrix}_{\alpha I},

(4.73)

\displaystyle[1^{I}|

\displaystyle=\sqrt{m_{1}}\begin{pmatrix}c_{1}^{2}e^{\frac{\eta_{1}}{2}}+|s_{1}|^{2}e^{-\frac{\eta_{1}}{2}}&c_{1}s^{*}_{1}(e^{\frac{\eta_{1}}{2}}-e^{-\frac{\eta_{1}}{2}})\\
c_{1}s_{1}(e^{\frac{\eta_{1}}{2}}-e^{-\frac{\eta_{1}}{2}})&|s_{1}|^{2}e^{\frac{\eta_{1}}{2}}+c_{1}^{2}e^{-\frac{\eta_{1}}{2}}\end{pmatrix}_{\,\,\dot{\alpha}}^{I}=\begin{pmatrix}a_{1}&b_{1}\\
b^{*}_{1}&c_{1}\end{pmatrix}_{\,\,\dot{\alpha}}^{I},

\displaystyle|2_{I}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sqrt{m_{2}}\begin{pmatrix}c^{2}_{2}e^{\frac{\eta_{2}}{2}}+|s_{2}|^{2}e^{-\frac{\eta_{2}}{2}}&c_{2}s^{*}_{2}(e^{\frac{\eta_{2}}{2}}-e^{-\frac{\eta_{2}}{2}})\\
c_{2}s_{2}(e^{\frac{\eta_{2}}{2}}-e^{-\frac{\eta_{2}}{2}})&|s_{2}|^{2}e^{\frac{\eta_{2}}{2}}+c^{2}_{2}e^{-\frac{\eta_{2}}{2}}\end{pmatrix}_{\alpha I}=\begin{pmatrix}a_{2}&b_{2}\\
b^{*}_{2}&c_{2}\end{pmatrix}_{\alpha I},

\displaystyle[2^{I}|

\displaystyle=\sqrt{m_{2}}\begin{pmatrix}c_{2}^{2}e^{\frac{\eta_{2}}{2}}+|s_{2}|^{2}e^{-\frac{\eta_{2}}{2}}&c_{2}s^{*}_{2}(e^{\frac{\eta_{2}}{2}}-e^{-\frac{\eta_{2}}{2}})\\
c_{2}s_{2}(e^{\frac{\eta_{2}}{2}}-e^{-\frac{\eta_{2}}{2}})&|s_{2}|^{2}e^{\frac{\eta_{2}}{2}}+c_{2}^{2}e^{-\frac{\eta_{2}}{2}}\end{pmatrix}_{\,\,\dot{\alpha}}^{I}=\begin{pmatrix}a_{2}&b_{2}\\
b^{*}_{2}&c_{2}\end{pmatrix}_{\,\,\dot{\alpha}}^{I},

\displaystyle|3_{I}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sqrt{m_{3}}\begin{pmatrix}c^{2}_{3}e^{\frac{\eta_{3}}{2}}+|s_{3}|^{2}e^{-\frac{\eta_{3}}{2}}&c_{3}s^{*}_{3}(e^{\frac{\eta_{3}}{2}}-e^{-\frac{\eta_{3}}{2}})\\
c_{3}s_{3}(e^{\frac{\eta_{3}}{2}}-e^{-\frac{\eta_{3}}{2}})&|s_{3}|^{2}e^{\frac{\eta_{3}}{2}}+c^{2}_{3}e^{-\frac{\eta_{3}}{2}}\end{pmatrix}_{\alpha I}=\begin{pmatrix}a_{3}&b_{3}\\
b^{*}_{3}&c_{3}\end{pmatrix}_{\alpha I},

\displaystyle[3^{I}|

\displaystyle=\sqrt{m_{3}}\begin{pmatrix}c_{3}^{2}e^{\frac{\eta_{3}}{2}}+|s_{3}|^{2}e^{-\frac{\eta_{3}}{2}}&c_{3}s^{*}_{3}(e^{\frac{\eta_{3}}{2}}-e^{-\frac{\eta_{3}}{2}})\\
c_{3}s_{3}(e^{\frac{\eta_{3}}{2}}-e^{-\frac{\eta_{3}}{2}})&|s_{3}|^{2}e^{\frac{\eta_{3}}{2}}+c_{3}^{2}e^{-\frac{\eta_{3}}{2}}\end{pmatrix}_{\,\,\dot{\alpha}}^{I}=\begin{pmatrix}a_{3}&b_{3}\\
b^{*}_{3}&c_{3}\end{pmatrix}_{\,\,\dot{\alpha}}^{I},

where
c=\cos\frac{\theta_{j}}{2},s=e^{i\phi_{j}}\sin\frac{\theta_{j}}{2},\eta_{j}=\text{arctanh}{\frac{|\mathbf{p}_{j}|}{E_{j}}}\quad(j=1,2,3)
. Using the antisymmetric tensors
\varepsilon_{\alpha\beta}
and
\varepsilon_{IJ}
(and their inverses) to raise and lower Lorentz and little group indices, the remaining spinors follow straightforwardly from the above definitions.

As an illustration, the amplitude in Eq. (
4.78
) can be written in explicit form as

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\{I^{(3)}_{1}\}}^{\{I^{(1)}_{1}\}}(0,\frac{1}{2})\propto

\displaystyle\langle 1^{I^{(1)}_{1}}3_{I^{(3)}_{1}}\rangle-[1^{I^{(1)}_{1}}3_{I^{(3)}_{1}}]

(4.74)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}c_{1}&-b_{1}\\
-b^{*}_{1}&a_{1}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}a_{3}&b_{3}\\
b^{*}_{3}&c_{3}\end{pmatrix}-\begin{pmatrix}a_{1}&b_{1}\\
b^{*}_{1}&c_{1}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}-c_{3}&b_{3}\\
b^{*}_{3}&-a_{3}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}c_{1}a_{3}+a_{1}c_{3}-2b_{1}b_{3}^{*}&c_{1}b_{3}-b_{1}c_{3}-a_{1}b_{3}+b_{1}a_{3}\\
a_{1}b^{*}_{3}-b_{1}^{*}a_{3}+b_{1}^{*}c_{3}-c_{1}b_{3}^{*}&a_{1}c_{3}+c_{1}a_{3}-2b_{1}^{*}b_{3}\end{pmatrix}^{I_{1}^{(1)}}_{\,\,I_{1}^{(3)}}.

In what follows, canonical-spinor variables are denoted in
BOLD
for brevity, and the
\mathrm{SU}(2)
little group indices are suppressed, since they are totally symmetric and would make the notation lengthy.

1.

s_{3}=\frac{1}{2},s_{1}=\frac{1}{2},s_{2}=0
. There are two
(L,S)
combinations:
(0,\frac{1}{2})
and
(1,\frac{1}{2})
.

(a)

L=0,S=\frac{1}{2}:

\mathcal{A}_{\{I^{(3)}_{1}\}}^{\{I^{(1)}_{1}\}}(0,\frac{1}{2})=\mathcal{S}_{\{K_{1}\}}^{\{I^{(1)}_{1}\}}\mathcal{Y}^{0}C_{\frac{1}{2},\{I^{(3)}_{1}\}}^{\frac{1}{2},\{K_{1}\};0},

(4.75)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{\{K_{1}\}}^{\{I^{(1)}_{1}\}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{m_{3}(E_{1\mathrm{com}}+m_{1})}}(\tau_{1}^{1})_{\{J_{1}\}}^{\{I_{1}^{(1)}\}}C_{\frac{1}{2},\{K_{1}\}}^{\frac{1}{2},\{J_{1}\};0}

(4.76)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{m_{3}(E_{1\mathrm{com}}+m_{1})}}(\langle\mathbf{13}\rangle-[\mathbf{13}]),

and

\mathcal{Y}^{0}=\sqrt{\frac{m_{3}}{\pi|\mathbf{p}_{1\mathrm{com}}|}}.

(4.77)

The amplitude is

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\{I^{(3)}_{1}\}}^{\{I^{(1)}_{1}\}}(0,\frac{1}{2})=\sqrt{\frac{m_{3}}{\pi|\mathbf{p}_{1\mathrm{com}}|}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{m_{3}(E_{1\mathrm{com}}+m_{1})}}(\langle\mathbf{13}\rangle-[\mathbf{13}]).

(4.78)

(b)

L=1,S=\frac{1}{2}:

\mathcal{A}_{\{I^{(3)}_{1}\}}^{\{I^{(1)}_{1}\}}(1,\frac{1}{2})=\mathcal{S}_{\{K_{1}\}}^{\{I^{(1)}_{1}\}}\mathcal{Y}^{1}_{\{L_{1}L_{2}\}}C_{\frac{1}{2},\{I^{(3)}_{1}\}}^{\frac{1}{2},\{K_{1}\};1,\{L_{1}L_{2}\}},

(4.79)

where
\mathcal{S}_{\{K_{1}\}}^{\{I^{(1)}_{1}\}}
can be obtained from Eq. (
4.76
) and

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}^{1}_{\{L_{1}L_{2}\}}=\sqrt{\frac{3}{2\pi|\mathbf{p}_{1\mathrm{com}}|^{3}m_{3}}}\langle\mathbf{3}|p_{1}|\mathbf{3}].

(4.80)

The amplitude is

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\{I^{(3)}_{1}\}}^{\{I^{(1)}_{1}\}}(1,\frac{1}{2})=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{3}{2\pi|\mathbf{p}_{1\mathrm{com}}|^{3}m_{3}}}\frac{(m_{1}+m_{2}+m_{3})(m_{3}+m_{1}-m_{2})}{2\sqrt{m_{3}(E_{1\mathrm{com}}+m_{1})}}\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}

(4.81)

\displaystyle\times(\langle\mathbf{13}\rangle+[\mathbf{13}]).

2.

s_{3}=0,s_{1}=\frac{1}{2},s_{2}=\frac{1}{2}
. There are two
(L,S)
combinations:
(0,0)
and
(1,1)
.

(a)

L=0,S=0:

\mathcal{A}^{\{I^{(1)}_{1}\},\{I^{(2)}_{1}\}}(0,0)=\mathcal{S}^{\{I^{(1)}_{1}\},\{I^{(2)}_{1}\}}\mathcal{Y}^{0}C_{0}^{0;0},

(4.82)

where
\mathcal{Y}^{0}
can be obtained from Eq. (
4.77
) and

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{\{I^{(1)}_{1}\},\{I^{(2)}_{1}\}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{m_{3}(E_{1\mathrm{com}}+m_{1})m_{3}(E_{2\mathrm{com}}+m_{2})}}(\tau_{1}^{1})_{\{J_{1}\}}^{\{I_{1}^{(1)}\}}(\tau_{2}^{1})_{\{N_{1}\}}^{\{I_{1}^{(2)}\}}C_{0}^{\frac{1}{2},\{J_{1}\};\frac{1}{2},\{N_{1}\}}

(4.83)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{m_{1}+m_{2}+m_{3}}{\sqrt{m_{3}(E_{1\mathrm{com}}+m_{1})m_{3}(E_{2\mathrm{com}}+m_{2})}}(\langle\mathbf{12}\rangle-[\mathbf{12}]).

The amplitude is

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\{I^{(1)}_{1}\},\{I^{(2)}_{1}\}}(0,0)=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{m_{3}}{\pi|\mathbf{p}_{1\mathrm{com}}|}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{m_{1}+m_{2}+m_{3}}{\sqrt{m_{3}(E_{1\mathrm{com}}+m_{1})m_{3}(E_{2\mathrm{com}}+m_{2})}}

(4.84)

\displaystyle\times(\langle\mathbf{12}\rangle-[\mathbf{12}]).

(b)

L=1,S=1:

\mathcal{A}^{\{I^{(1)}_{1}\},\{I^{(2)}_{1}\}}(1,1)=\mathcal{S}^{\{I^{(1)}_{1}\},\{I^{(2)}_{1}\}}_{\{K_{1}K_{2}\}}\mathcal{Y}^{1}_{\{L_{1}L_{2}\}}C_{0}^{1,\{K_{1}K_{2}\};1,\{L_{1}L_{2}\}},

(4.85)

where
\mathcal{Y}^{1}_{\{L_{1}L_{2}\}}
can be obtained from Eq. (
